# Private owned HTTP Chat staging

### Hosted4e8 consumer diagnosis — fixture gate context, production cause unproved

Exact hosted source: `4e8c68a1f0dda9bdabd2ede26153be7914fb7074`.
[Consumer run42999415454, job130451631759](https://github.com/openclaw/clawrouter/actions/runs/42999415454/job/130451631759)
started Workerd successfully. The normal pinned-client suite passed7 tests;
each of the3 custody client invocations passed its failed-delivery test. The
outer harness then failed its all-completed-roots-success assertion and the
provider-install/retry-cancel/new-post-install logical-ACK predicates. These
client results do not prove accounting or retirement success.

The supplied Workerd stack identifies Object.accepted, injected immediately
after acceptOwnedHttpBudgetReservation's actual leg acceptance. The fixture
constructs failureGate/sendGate/cancelGate in createOwnedBudgetSequenceFixture
during the separate setup POST. accepted() resolves failureGate after the last
expected accepted leg even when options.reserveFailure is absent. In all three
reported schedules the options are legs4/providerfireworks, so the only
`await failureGate` branch is unreachable. The warning therefore has a concrete
fixture-created Promise and expired setup context; it does not establish that
a production acquisition or logical ACK was canceled by that particular gate.

The same source-context defect exists in unused setup gates: the terminal's
lateGate and ACK fixture's readGate/uploadGate are constructed by their setup
POSTs. DELETE cleanup resolves them along with the budget's three gates.
Per-reader ACK closed/unlock gates are different: they are constructed when
the inference reader is acquired and are intentional physical holds. They must
remain held until the existing release point. The warning on cleanup release
must not be treated as a genuine application retirement receipt.

This is not an established expected failure of disabled R_failed replacement.
These cases configure response retry, not the five-code credential-producer
failure path. No new-family manifest refusal flag is installed in the tested
control flow. Acceptance reaching the injected budget hook also proves that
the initial request/plan admission was not uniformly refused. Later admission
or production failure remains possible; neither is proven by the bare timeout.

**Authorized fixture repair, implemented; runtime UNRUN.** Each existing named
gate API now represents an unused gate with its release fact only. Its
Promise/resolver is created lazily in the originating inference operation when
that operation first needs the hold. A release before first use records that
fact without constructing/resolving a setup-context Promise; first use observes
an already released gate consistently. Budget failure awaits stay inside the
returned fixture fetch Promise, send/cancel holds remain in their returned
operation chains, terminal late response stays in tracked wrapOriginal work,
and ACK read/upload holds stay in the accepted read/upload receipts. Per-reader
closed/unlock gates and their physical release conditions are unchanged.
Every real hold, original method call, cleanup join and UNKNOWN assertion
remains. No compatibility flag, timeout, profile, production code or retirement
rule changes. Only the six named setup gates above receive this repair.

The assertions now include their missing bounded failure evidence. The
completed-root assertion includes existing id/origin/undefined scalars for
failed rows. The ACK deadline messages include cuts/retirements, per-ACK state
bits, terminal logical count/pending/phase bits, budget accepted/reserve/
dispatch/settle/ingest counts, provider POST count and diagnostic origins.
They do not dump response bodies, headers,
authorization, raw reasons, request IDs, URLs or the whole custody snapshot.
The same exact13-cut/one-logical/no-pending condition and all subsequent
physical/debit/stale checks remain. This is meaningful observed-state evidence,
not an implementation-text fixture or permission to accept a failed predicate.

The first test already collects bounded origins per completed request but its
assertion message does not expose the failing row. The ACK test similarly loses
which of its three predicate terms failed. Consequently this source/log packet
proves the setup-gate defect and the diagnostic gap, not a complete causal
repair for the observed stalls or failed completed root. A new exact-head hosted
run of this fixture patch must distinguish those remaining cases. No local
runtime, test execution, rerun, flag change or production patch was performed.
Source-only raw/generated grammar, privacy and whitespace are the available
checks; runtime/typecheck remain UNRUN. ROOT owns further hosted polling and
publication authority. Production source and its reviewed STATIC debit are
unchanged by this fixture-only repair.

#### Construct-reference reconciliation and reproducible census

The941578 proposal omitted one separately charged Construct-code reference for
each of the694 ordinary module declarations. This is the same relationship
already included in the adopted pinned adapter row. The original function
macro owns its fixed target, declaration binding and captured scope link; it
does not substitute for this additional MakeConstructor-installed code
reference. Add P694=5552 bytes, with no new definition target, string name or
primitive occurrence: **S becomes947130**, and the ordinary-prototype row is
694*(C2/P8/N1/U20)=88832.

MakeConstructor also sets ConstructorKind to base. That existing ordinary
function slot belongs to the frozen function-target macro (Table25);
MakeConstructor changes its value rather than creating a second slot. This
correction does not reprice that macro's ordinary slots, attributes or
prototype metadata already assigned elsewhere. In contrast, OrdinaryFunctionCreate
explicitly creates no Construct internal method; MakeConstructor installs its
code reference. The pinned adapter's112-byte row plus its16-byte outside-macro
PrivateElements correction gives the same128-byte relationship envelope.
No second pinned correction is added. The shared Construct algorithm target
is borrowed; only its extra reference is charged here.
[ECMA2026 OrdinaryFunctionCreate and MakeConstructor](https://tc39.es/ecma262/2026/multipage/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours.html#sec-makeconstructor).

```javascript
function moduleItems(s,d) {

const priorDelta=JSON.parse(result);

const keywords=new Set("async await break case catch class const continue debugger default delete do else export extends finally for function if import in instanceof let new of return super switch this throw try typeof var void while with yield get set static".split(" "));
const scalar=new Set(["true","false","null","undefined","NaN","Infinity"]);
const global=[],details=[];
for(const f of parsed){
 const t=f.curD.t,mask=new Set(),macro=new Set(),bc=(()=>{try{return bindingCensus(f.curD);}catch(e){throw Error(f.path+' '+e.message);}})();
 for(const d of f.add){for(let j=d.start;j<=d.end;j++)mask.add(j);if(d.kind==='function')macro.add(d.start+1);else if(d.kind==='class')macro.add(d.start+1);else if(d.kind!=='arrow')macro.add(d.start);}
 for(const v of f.newVars){for(let j=v.start;j<v.end;j++)mask.add(j);}
 for(const field of f.newFields){for(let j=field.start;j<=field.end;j++)mask.add(j);macro.add(field.start+(field.static?1:0));}
 for(const r of priorDelta.filter(r=>r.path===f.path))for(const [a,b]of r.ranges)for(let j=a;j<=b;j++)mask.add(j);
 for(const v of f.newVars){const i=f.curD.t.findIndex((x,i)=>x.v===v.name&&f.curD.parent[i]===-1&&['let','const','var'].includes(f.curD.t[i-1]?.v));macro.add(i);}

 const codeBodies=new Set(f.curD.ds.map(d=>d.body).filter(i=>t[i]?.v==='{'));
 const objectBodies=new Set();
 for(let i=0;i<t.length;i++)if(t[i].v==='{'&&!codeBodies.has(i)&&!bc.patternKeys.has(i)&&['=','return','(', '[', ',',':','?','||','&&','??'].includes(t[i-1]?.v))objectBodies.add(i);

 const bound=new Set([...bc.binding].map(i=>t[i].v));
 for(const d of f.curD.ds)if(['function','class'].includes(d.kind))bound.add(d.name);
 for(const i of f.curM.imports)bound.add(i.name);
 for(const v of f.curM.vars)bound.add(v.name);
 bound.add('arguments');
 const globals=Object.create(null);
 const groups=Object.create(null),items=[];
 function add(k,j){const q=groups[k]??={n:0,u:0};q.n++;if(['quoted','template','property','identifier','binding','reference','global','regex'].includes(k))q.u+=t[j].v.length;items.push([k,j,t[j].v]);}
 for(const j of [...mask].sort((a,b)=>a-b)){
 const x=t[j];if(macro.has(j))continue;
 if(x.k==='str')add('quoted',j);
 else if(x.k==='quasi')add('template',j);
 else if(x.k==='regex')add('regex',j);
 else if(x.k==='num')add('number',j);
 else if(x.k==='id'){
 const prop=['.','?.'].includes(t[j-1]?.v)||(t[j+1]?.v===':'&&['{',','].includes(t[j-1]?.v))||bc.patternKeys.has(j)||(objectBodies.has(f.curD.parent[j])&&['{',','].includes(t[j-1]?.v)&&[',','}'].includes(t[j+1]?.v));
 if(prop)add('property',j);
 if(bc.binding.has(j))add('binding',j);
 else if(!prop&&['true','false','null'].includes(x.v))add('scalar',j);
 else if(!prop&&!keywords.has(x.v)){if(!bound.has(x.v)){add('global',j);globals[x.v]=(globals[x.v]??0)+1;}else add('reference',j);}
 }
 }
 global.push({path:f.path,groups,globals,tokens:mask.size});
 details.push({path:f.path,items});
}


import {createHash} from 'node:crypto';
const codeSort=(a,b)=>JSON.stringify(a)<JSON.stringify(b)?-1:JSON.stringify(a)>JSON.stringify(b)?1:0;
const digest=items=>createHash('sha256').update(JSON.stringify([...items].sort(codeSort))).digest('hex');
const inputHash=createHash('sha256');
for(const f of parsed) inputHash.update(f.path+'\0'+f.raw+'\0');
const prototypes=[];
for(const f of parsed) for(const d of f.add)
 if(d.kind==='function'&&!d.parent&&f.curD.t[d.start-1]?.v!=='async'&&f.curD.t[d.start+1]?.v!=='*')
  prototypes.push([f.path,d.name,d.line]);
const groups=Object.create(null),metadata=[];
for(const f of details)for(const[k,j,v]of f.items)if(!['reference','regex'].includes(k)){
 const file=parsed.find(x=>x.path===f.path),token=file.curD.t[j];
 metadata.push([f.path,k,token.s,v]);
 const g=groups[k]??={N:0,U:0};g.N++;if(!['number','scalar'].includes(k))g.U+=v.length;
}
let N=0,U=0;for(const g of Object.values(groups)){N+=g.N;U+=g.U;}
const perModule=parsed.map(f=>({path:f.path,
 prototypes:prototypes.filter(x=>x[0]===f.path).length,
 operands:metadata.filter(x=>x[0]===f.path).length,
 metadataHash:digest(metadata.filter(x=>x[0]===f.path))
}));
console.log(JSON.stringify({
 head,base,files:parsed.length,inputHash:inputHash.digest('hex'),
 prototypes:{count:prototypes.length,rosterHash:digest(prototypes)},
 metadata:{N,U,bytes:8*N+2*U,rosterHash:digest(metadata),groups},
 delta:{scopes:priorDelta.length,tokens:priorDelta.reduce((s,r)=>s+r.ranges.reduce((n,[a,b])=>n+b-a+1,0),0)},
 perModule
}));
// Optional, bounded module-local evidence; no artifact is written.
// CENSUS_MODULE must be one exact path in the 32-module input list.
if(process.env.CENSUS_MODULE){
 const path=process.env.CENSUS_MODULE;
 if(!parsed.some(f=>f.path===path))throw Error('not a census module');
 const offset=Number(process.env.CENSUS_OFFSET??0), limit=Number(process.env.CENSUS_LIMIT??100);
 if(!Number.isSafeInteger(offset)||offset<0||!Number.isSafeInteger(limit)||limit<1||limit>200)throw Error('invalid page');
 const rows=metadata.filter(x=>x[0]===path), proto=prototypes.filter(x=>x[0]===path);
 console.log(JSON.stringify({path,offset,limit,prototypeCount:proto.length,operandCount:rows.length,
  prototypes:proto.slice(offset,offset+limit),operands:rows.slice(offset,offset+limit)}));
}
```

### STATIC_CANONICAL_OWNER_REDESIGN_PLAN — unimplemented, not closed

Source anchor: code checkpoint `d11be558307c7ce6171bebcb249e434f9e8d158f`.
The exact28 work is complete. This section proposes a replacement ownership
layout and records why its currently attributable reductions do not close the
fixed65536 gate. It authorizes no implementation, new admission or retirement.
It is not a claim that all possible implementations are impossible.

**Proposed canonical layout.** Keep the existing `proxy-completion` runtime
leaf, its2931-byte prefix, body-budget-only runtime dependency, three canonical
registration maps and fixed receiver lookups. Keep the existing named request
and revalidation receiver methods; their guards are an authority boundary, not
duplicate targets to erase. Place the private HTTP-operation implementation
closure in the existing `http-identity-owner.ts`, alongside its request, upload,
sink, budget, terminal, archive, normalization and publication state. Move the
following private state and its implementation together, retaining each current
registry identity and each current counter/once field:

| Source private owner | State moved with its private operations | Canonical association retained |
| --- | --- | --- |
| proxy | invocation, preparation receiver, prepared selection/upload and retry ports | ownedProxyInvocations, ownedPreparationReceivers, ownedPreparedSelections, ownedPreparedUploads, ownedProxyCount |
| proxy-accounting | private accounting command/physical/logical state | ownedAccountingStates, ownedAccountingRequests, ownedAccountingRequestCount |
| proxy-response | codec consumers, private accepted usage and their concrete readers | codecConsumers, codecConsumerCount, ownedAcceptedUsage; exact child/Response/output associations |
| soft-budget | prepared private controller and status lifecycle | ownedSoftBudgets; exact plan, operation, attempt and independent start/cleanup positions |
| venice-chat-runtime | private codec/source role and retirement | ownedCodecs, ownedCodecCount; existing revalidation and role identities |

These are proposed moves of the private additions, not copies, new registries,
request-only replacements or a common opcode owner. Ordinary exports, common
parsing/framing algorithms and their existing state remain in their current
modules. The four frozen files, DO producers/schema, all profiles and default
activation stay outside the move. Shared ordinary lexical helpers cannot be
copied into this unit: their existing callable boundary must remain or its
additional export/import reference must appear on the replacement side.
A moved function or class earns **zero target credit**.

The private credential/preflight/cache/source owners remain separate canonical
domains in this first candidate. In particular, the existing foreground map,
selected/authentication parents, cache-run registry and actual source receipt
remain authoritative. Moving HTTP operations does not turn a current request
into authority to claim a foreign credential or replace the four actual grant
binding reads. The preclaim, truthful partial adoption, failed-producer
certificates, five-code receipt, status-start and sequence-wide budget repairs
remain part of the replacement contract.

**Concrete fusion rule.** Delete an internal checker/funder/forwarder only by
placing its statements in a particular existing producer command at the old
call position. Preserve lookup count, argument evaluation, short circuits,
funding before factories, and the command's own return/throw kind. Examples
which define this boundary, not yet credited deletions, are:

| Existing internal layer | Existing command where its exact checks belong |
| --- | --- |
| matchesOwnedHttpConfigurationPreparation | request receiver admitOwnedHttpConfigurationCommand, retaining its provider-permit alternative |
| matchesOwnedHttpInitialSelection | checkOwnedHttpInitialSelection, after its existing request check |
| matchesOwnedHttpProviderFetchInput | startOwnedHttpProviderFetch, before the actual provider command factory/method |
| matchesOwnedHttpBudgetPlan | prepareOwnedHttpAccountingTerminalPlan, retaining exact accounting, plan, upload, prepared mapping and handoff phase |
| matchesOwnedHttpArchiveInput / claimOwnedHttpArchiveInput | archive preparation checks and the existing once claim; preserve both current checks at their existing positions |

Archive validation still needs the admitted tuple after retry. The public
archive-preparation command and later put/pipe/cancel/abort commands remain
distinct. A predicate shared by both validation and claiming cannot be counted
as removed until both uses are replaced and duplicated literal/definition
products are counted. The same applies to public-body and revalidation
matchers used by more than one physical owner. A membership check cannot
replace their Response/body/signal/attempt/plan comparisons.

**Required semantic and execution accounting.** Any implementation of this
layout must keep source-specific original handlers, their frozen shape,
FIRST-before-payload behavior, native correlation and independent installation
origins. Keep codec VALUE-before-DONE, generic finished/EOF skipping, normalizer
DONE-before-VALUE and intake's three VALUE observations as separate algorithms.
No mode selector, callback bag or merged receipt protocol replaces them.
The three acquisition owners keep their different started gates, undefined
facts, VOID delivery and failed-source certificates.

Synchronous fusion must snapshot each old operand before its old callee would
run; a receiver/registry lookup cannot move ahead of those operands. Internal
early returns must become local control flow without returning from an outer
finally or changing a pending throw. A class method's this, arguments and
super/new.target context cannot be substituted for a removed ordinary function
without an explicit equivalence and environment row. Keep every async result,
await and caller alias until its actual original consumer; moving an async body
into a caller would change Promise identity/adoption and is not a free fusion.
Do not move declaration evaluation across captured intrinsics, class heritage,
module initialization or the shared parser; those changes can recreate startup
cycles and hoist/TDZ failures. Ordinary low-level modules still reach private
commands only through the existing runtime leaf.

Every changed method/callee frame, operand snapshot, arguments object, block,
capture and reentrant overlap needs a successor ACTIVE128/SYNC512 table.
Persistent receipts, source/caller contexts and native products keep their V2
owners; no STATIC-to-I/O transfer or UNKNOWN refund is implied. Genuine subject
probes must keep their current inner-check reach and separate wrong-this cases.
All existing failed/preclaim/status/budget and observer/archive fixtures carry
forward; relocation is not permission to weaken their hooks or assertions.

**Disposition and minimum next design boundary.** This concrete canonical-unit
candidate is rejected as a closing plan before implementation. Co-location plus
module-level checker/funder/forwarder fusion cannot supply a closed replacement
roster on its own. The minimum additional structural boundary is the actual
internal class-member/initializer/closure layer, not another import spelling
or shared three-method base: existing named producer methods would have to own
more of their internal phase/check/funding statements while deleting their old
method/initializer/function products. Externally retained handler targets and
semantically distinct capture/receipt protocols remain protected.

No such complete deletion-and-replacement roster has been established here.
Its numeric requirement is explicit: at least1178 plus all other omitted
STATIC and every mandatory helper/import retained even after the deliberately
unattainable universal subtraction above; the realizable deficit is larger.
A successor must name those actual member products and every replacement,
including literals, rather than apply the630/668 maxima as credits. This is
the current precise plan obstruction, not an unimplemented fit certificate.
No further code consolidation, family admission or failed-ticket replacement
is enabled. Runtime/typecheck and whole STATIC/ACTIVE/SYNC/DATA/COMMON remain
OPEN.

#### Root retirement tail — source-derived lifetime obstruction

The current source has an explicit last-consumer boundary still to close.
In finishOwnedHttpRequest, the clean path deletes its request registry entry
and calls owner.funding.release(), then may construct and throw
AggregateError(failures,"owned HTTP request retirement"). Its async result also
has the separately installed suppression pair:
void owner.retirement.then(() => undefined, () => undefined).

A clean physical upload rejection is a concrete path: UploadOwner.rejected
records bounded metadata without setting UNKNOWN when recording succeeds;
retirement copies that record into failures, joins upload/sink cleanup and
can reach the clean release with failures nonempty. Thus UNKNOWN retention
does not make the post-release aggregate path unreachable.

The retained failures vector/metadata, aggregate construction/result, root
async result, dependent and queued suppression work are real custody products.
The two APP arrows also share finishOwnedHttpRequest's lexical owner/cap/
arguments environment; an unused lexical reference is not a source-level
severance proof. Even the successful undefined-result path has the suppression
tail. The later waitUntil registration is not a separate prepaid APP owner:
registerOwnedHttpRetirement checks the same root and same retirement Promise.

Consequently a larger initial Wroot alone does not repair this release point.
The complete manifest needs a genuine prepaid post-retirement ownership and
last-consumer cut, preserving logical accounting, physical joins, inert-cell
trust and UNKNOWN. No lifetime/category transfer, late reserve, new registry
or permanent per-request leak is assumed. This packet makes no source change
and supplies no release authority.

Separate shared-kernel interfaces are explicit: createOwnedHttpRequest has
three actual/formal arguments; tryBeginOwnedHttpRequest has two;
finishOwnedHttpRequest has one; the retirement async arrow has zero; both
new-Promise executors have one formal but receive two native resolver
arguments; each zero-formal suppression arrow receives one reaction argument.
The existing constructors' APP/P0/R components are retained, not replaced by
another K executor or repriced by an unreviewed kernel. Root diagnostic
collection, AggregateError/error-native construction, source suspension,
callback/caller overlap and ACTIVE/SYNC remain OPEN.

#### ROOT_RETIREMENT_TAIL_REPAIR_PLAN — exact custody edge, not code authority

Source: `8abfe6e4b1aa676d2725db9323aa551750a8e4e8`. The root retirement source
is unchanged from the published diagnostic. This plan chooses the existing
HTTP owner as the payer through all retirement work. It does not borrow the
terminal, caller or shared registry debit. A final native-tail custody boundary
is required before a refundable implementation can be specified completely.

**Actual phases and products.** The suppression installation at source line4778
runs when finishOwnedHttpRequest first returns, before its async body resumes
past the completion await. Thus the dependent/reactions are already allocated
before the later release at4773; they are not factories sequenced after that
release merely because their source line is later. The AggregateError factory
at4776 actually executes after release on the clean physical-failure path.

| Phase | Still-owned products and required cut |
| --- | --- |
| Entry and registration | One retirement async result; one async APP function and its source environment; the two suppression APP arrows sharing finishOwnedHttpRequest's environment; the suppression dependent/reactions/job; the exact index-local retirement reference and registerOwnedHttpRetirement argument. Register the same Promise once through the existing actual waitUntil call. |
| Joins and cleanup | Completion, upload joined/retired and sink receipts; root continuation/await work; failures array and each referenced failure; diagnostic/reporting operands; existing consumers and parents until their actual joins. UNKNOWN preserves every existing debit and ownership association. |
| Outcome construction | On failure, one AggregateError and its distinct errors array, constructor iterable/descriptor work, the input failures vector and referenced failure targets, source throw Completion, and the async Promise rejection value. On success the result is undefined, but source/native result work remains. |
| Suppression dispatch | Exactly one selected handler executes. The other handler still exists until its actual reaction ownership ends. The selected job retains the incoming aggregate or undefined, the handler/environment and dependent capability. A cleared request registry does not clear those references. |
| Native postlude | After the handler returns, NewPromiseReactionJob still invokes the dependent capability's Resolve/Reject and returns through the job/host invocation. These products are included by the existing paid-I/O lifetime rule. |

The last row follows the ordering in
[ECMA2026 NewPromiseReactionJob](https://tc39.es/ecma262/2026/multipage/control-abstraction-objects.html#sec-newpromisereactionjob).
This is an ownership inference from those steps, not a claim about host heap
collection. The root's release runs before even its own async outcome has
settled; neither settlement nor an empty suppression body proves that postlude
has ended.

**Existing payer candidates.** RequestOwner.funding is the only current
prospective owner of this whole tail and must remain positive. The accounting
terminal and ProxyCompletion have already delivered the joins that let the root
continue; transferring the tail backward would create an unproved second
lifetime. index.fetch can return the response while the root is pending; its
local retirement reference has no independent tail lease. Shared registryFunding
is released when requestCount reaches zero and has no independently admitted
tail membership. Keeping it alive would require a real additional association
and its own last-consumer proof, not a reference to the existing1024 allowance.
The existing waitUntil registration supplies no APP completion receipt after
its argument's final job. Its documented role is keeping the invocation alive
for supplied work, not acknowledging memory retirement.
[Workers context API](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/runtime-apis/context/#waituntil).

**Prospective manifest interface.** Before the first root/receipt factory, the
existing request admission must cover the following disjoint ownership rows:

```text
W_root_tail(f) =
    K_retirement + T_suppression
  + E_retirement_function_and_source_contexts
  + E_two_handlers_and_shared_finish_environment
  + W_root_awaits_and_resume_work
  + W_failures_vector(f) + W_failure_targets_not_paid_elsewhere(f)
  + W_aggregate_target_and_errors_vector(f)
  + W_aggregate_constructor_and_outcome_overlap(f)
  + W_registration_and_actual_caller_reference_positions
  + W_uncovered_source_entry_and_final_native_postlude
```

K_retirement=1084 and T_suppression=1628 give a2712-byte native component,
not a tail upper. K already contains its result/resolvers; T already contains
its dependent capability/reactions/job target. Add neither R nor another P0 to
those same products. The source async function, APP arrows/environments,
executing operands, aggregate and caller edges are additional. Shared finish
environment targets are counted once, with every actual capture/reference edge
counted. f is the complete root failure-position union, not elapsed history;
its current complete census is still OPEN. The ordinary HttpError construction
proposal does not price AggregateError's iterable/errors-array constructor.
No Error48, requestMetadataBytes or other component supplies these missing rows.

Kernel interfaces remain finish(1/1), async arrow(0/0), selected suppression
handler(1/0), registration(3/3), actual waitUntil(1 argument), and
AggregateError(2 arguments: failures array and the29-unit fixed message).
Native result/capability targets stay in K/T; parameter binding, APP contexts,
aggregate construction, source/await execution and postlude operands must be
matched against the reviewed kernels without adding their contained targets.
ACTIVE128/SYNC512 and any source/static delta remain separate. This formula is
an explicit OPEN admission interface, not an implemented or numeric upper.

**Precise preventing constraint.** Delaying release until a newly added
then/finally handler runs only moves the same problem: that handler and its
dependent/native job still outlive the release instruction. A queueMicrotask
cleanup also owns a callback/job while it releases. The current API has no
trusted after-final-job release notification, and a permanent per-request
residual debit would accumulate instead of retiring. Moving AggregateError
construction earlier repairs only its constructor ordering. It does not close
the native postlude or retained result.

The smallest required contract boundary is a specifically reviewed
**root final inert-receipt/native-postlude handoff**. It must identify the exact
finished Promise/result and remaining dependent/job objects eligible for
external custody, after all application owner/parent/registry/payload edges and
rights have been severed, with no further APP access, callback, allocation or
effect authorized by those artifacts. It must also state how the last release
activation and its remaining references are paid; naming only a post-handler
cut cannot implement that cut from inside the handler. The existing discharged
external-observer-cell rule does not already grant this root-result exception.
Any retained outcome must have a proved bounded graph and actual alias cuts;
calling it a receipt does not establish either. A new host acknowledgement
would be an alternative runtime interface, outside the current source lease.

No such handoff is adopted here. Until an exact contract is reviewed, retain the
current code for audit and keep W_root_tail/admission/whole-fit OPEN. Do not
implement an early release, new observer chain, fresh registry, permanent
per-request charge, reduced failure projection, or caller/STATIC/ACTIVE loan.
The allowed future implementation must preserve the same retirement Promise,
failure order and presence, original waitUntil order, logical accounting,
physical joins, spent retirement latch and unconditional UNKNOWN hold.

Required eventual witnesses use the actual request: clean success; clean
bounded upload rejection with nonempty aggregate; held completion/upload/sink;
registration failure; reporting failure; unrelated global UNKNOWN; and repeated
finish before retirement. They must observe a positive debit through every
owned constructor/handler/postlude, exact once release only at the reviewed cut,
and unchanged outcome identities/order. These are plan requirements, not new
authored runtime assertions or executed tests.

#### Actual caller/readiness lifetime proof

`startDriverRead` first rejects an existing pending read or `readObservation`,
then constructs the one current readiness receipt. `advance` consumes the
observed FIRST and attempts cell discharge before resolving/rejecting readiness.
An installation-uncertain cell cannot discharge, so a caller's next attempt
fails before another readiness factory. A logical failure without FIRST keeps
the original nonce/current receipt. No caller may infer physical retirement from
that prompt logical rejection.

All reached read callers have the following source-defined sequencing:

| Caller / genuine role | Current source sequence | Last-use and successor boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CodecReader or OwnedNormalizerReader during `sniffSse` | One `await operation.wait(readConsumer(...))` or `await operation.wait(httpReader.read())` per loop | The returned wrapper is not stored in a collection. Its actual pending-handler path removes the abort listener before resolving/rejecting; the loop then processes capture. Failure exits the loop and starts existing retirement. |
| Same readers during `readLimited` | The same one-at-a-time wait chain | Decode/accounting happens after await and before the next read. Its finally starts retirement; no detached read retry is added. |
| OwnedNormalizerReader replay | `pull -> beginDriverPull -> ownDriverPull(next(controller))`; next either emits an existing prefix/capture or awaits one read | Sniffing transfers only after its last wait returned. Prefix/capture branches create no read receipt. A subsequent native pull passes the base's prior-pull discharge barrier. |
| CodecReader in OwnedChatUsage | One guarded next activation; prefix/crossing branches or one awaited `readConsumer` | The same sniff reader can transfer into usage; the transfer does not reacquire closed or overlap the earlier sniff loop. A replacement uses a distinct genuine reader and remains additive. |
| GenericUsageReader in OwnedGenericChatUsage | One guarded next activation and one `await state.read()` | Finished checks precede acceptance; capture clears in finally. Stop/UNKNOWN does not authorize another read. |
| OwnedPublicReader | One guarded next activation and one `await startDriverRead()` | It publishes or closes after await, then returns its pull result. Retirement does not await the enclosing pull from inside that pull. |
| OwnedVeniceSource JSON | One `await source.read()` per loop, then copy and capture-finally | The loop cannot advance to another await before the previous continuation executes. EOF joins source retirement before subsequent JSON work. |
| OwnedVeniceSource SSE | One guarded OwnedVeniceEvents next loop; reads are sequential, with optional authority await between accepted frames | No second source read occurs during that authority await. Current pull/revalidation/parent work remain separately live and funded. |
| OwnedRevalidationRpc | Await fetch-ready, open once, then one `await startDriverRead(backing)` per loop | No driver pull is created. Finally joins retired before releasing RPC aliases; UNKNOWN can hold that whole caller. |

This proves the **current plus immediately preceding readiness-generation**
allowance for the fixed callers, not an arbitrary exported-reader usage model.
Native Promise reactions resume a caller in a later job. When that resumed
caller creates the next readiness Promise, the preceding await/current job may
still retain its old receipt. The caller's next await yields before a third
read can resolve and resume it. No loop saves old readiness identities. On the
failure route the retained observation blocks a successor instead of creating
an unbounded sequence of uncertain receipts.

For `HttpOperation.wait`, the source adds a real Promise/executor, abort closure
and signal registration, two pending handlers and their dependent, plus the
outer caller await. If abort wins, the pending handlers may survive the logical
wrapper; the read/loop exits and its retained cell still prevents fresh reads.
Those wrapper products are a **caller component** and are not the base's second
readiness Promise. Repeated healthy waits end their listener/handler use before
the next loop iteration; the executing previous reaction/current await overlap
still belongs in the native/caller envelope.

For the three private sniff/readLimited call sites, `wait` receives a genuine
VOID readiness Promise and at most the second `"upstream"` argument. The
optional discard parameter is undefined. Its actual APP function/environment

continuation can resume that same method. UNKNOWN does not discharge either
generation or the writer's existing physical originals.

### In-place ACK and logical-owner phase cuts

This source repair follows local partial commit
`321e1ed0636c4df2c83a7a34b30ca7c5e0dc9275`. It changes only the already-private
HTTP-owner/proxy implementation and focused fixtures. No new tail object,
registry, terminal shell, Promise, physical child, arena or funding allowance is
introduced by compaction. Every ACK record is fresh, enrolled at the existing
command/terminal position before `getReader`, and remains that same physical
record until retirement. ACK/data charges do not change at a phase cut.

The ACK's initial physical destination is the actual request/budget sequence or
exact terminal slot. A standalone logical adapter remains linked only through
its receipt projection and fetch handoff. `admittedOwnedBudgetCommand` and
`admittedOwnedLedgerCommand` synchronously return `command.ready`; their frames
have ended before queued ACK processing. The result Promise and projected
receipt retain no adapter link. Once logical projection, successful fetch
installation and callback/processing tails have ended, the fetch observation
severs and the ACK can delete its parent link. Receipt storage remains paid;
this is not a Promise-settlement retirement claim.

`AckReadCell` replaces the unused ACK read-ready Promise/executor/resolver nonce.
Each actual read gets a fresh two-field identity cell before invocation. The
current-cell/pending/FIRST checks precede DONE/VALUE access. Callback-active and
installation state remain distinct. Read processing clears the cell only after
the immutable capture's consumers have returned; the next queued processing
turn starts any successor. Old externally retained handlers then refer only to
an inert cell. An installation throw, including after FIRST or `throw undefined`,
keeps the cell and complete future capture state funded. Actual observer call
returns and unlock fulfillment values are ignored.

After read and FIRSTcancel have joined, unlock has been invoked and its
installation/receiver frames have returned, the same ACK deletes active
reader/decoder/capture/text/counter/origin/adapter fields. It retains these own
fields: `owner`, `body`, `closedState`, `closedOrigin`, `unlockState`,
`unlockOrigin`, `joinState`, `nativeFailure`; a terminal ACK also retains `slot`.
Closed and unlock each keep FIRST, installation, callback-tail and inert state.
Their four handlers bind only this never-reused ACK. Native closed qualification
and the shared read/cancel/closed failure record remain explicit; the referenced
record and registry products are additional to this field list. Independent
failures, even equal or undefined reasons, remain separate from native
correlation. No unknown installation is compacted into a healthy tail.

Retirement requires both independent roles and the post-unlock body-lock check.
A throwing/locked body check retains UNKNOWN ownership. The same physical
position is discharged once; payload and native-failure aliases clear before
its existing DATA debit ends when the HTTP owner is known; UNKNOWN retains the debit. External callbacks check inert state before any
owner lookup or payload inspection. A failed retirement cannot replay the
physical decrement. Source/fetch/read/cancel/unlock/closed originals and their
observer tails remain independent; logical receipt completion never joins an
unrelated original.

Other cuts occur at their existing irreversible boundaries:

- Budget phase4 revokes every leg registry entry, deletes the leg/plan/binding
  fields, and leaves the same sequence with `root`, `phase`, `physical` until its
  remaining commands retire. It ends only the logical sentinel, not an ACK.
- Retry closure runs after final feedback/invalidation and joined preparation,
  before delivery. It deletes ports, exclusions and obsolete prepared mappings,
  ends the local failed-key alias, and leaves an explicit closed latch. The
  chosen prepared-to-upload mapping survives for generic/codec/publication
  matchers. Full grants, cache borrowers, selected parents, generic retained
  funding and Venice/GMI32MiB future backing are unchanged.
- The terminal deletes physically retired operation-array properties. Event,
  reservation, sinks and custody are deleted only on a queued turn after launch
  and receipt-return frames, all logical lanes, diagnostics and logical
  resolution have finished. The physical shell, live positions, once guards,
  result/retirement Promises and required resolvers remain. UNKNOWN late-FIRST
  response disposition uses the same prepaid position. Successful accounting
  adoption clears request handoff aliases after producer acknowledgement;
  FAILED_PRE_ADOPTION keeps its evidence.

Fixed placement is prescribed before effects; it is not an overflow allocator:

| Window | Source-defined responsibility |
|---|---|
| OPERATION | Current request/attempt, logical claim/adoption, budget/retry rights before their cuts; physical destination/terminal-slot association |
| SOURCE | Acquisition/fetch/queue/pipe and handoff records; ACK instance/body and acquisition-installed closed handlers/receiver links |
| READ | Fresh read identity/callbacks, capture/decoder state, native qualification and shared correlation target; active read operands |
| TERMINAL | Cancel/unlock/closed rights and origins, unlock handlers/receiver links, same-position physical join and live terminal positions |
| SYNC | Actual current activations, call arguments, descriptors, reentry and old/new transition overlap only |

Persistent async products never move into SYNC or paid payload traversal.
Shared failure records, map/registry targets, native originals/reactions,
retained ready Promises, active cells, transition overlap, invocation,
credential, usage, publication and accounting owners must be added to this
placement. The eight/nine own-field lists and the reviewed333-product healthy
structural subset are not a window or combined upper bound. There is no new
1024-product certificate, enlarged window, debit refund or early upload cut.
The complete per-window, DATA/COMMON, paid-workspace and static65536 proofs
remain OPEN for the new immutable candidate.

Authored Node and exact-Workerd witnesses hold thirteen ACK closed/unlock
originals plus upload work while accounting completes promptly; another variant
holds five active final reads. They inspect actual own keys, unchanged charge
and physical counts at each cut, exactly one final decrement, stale external

shared/undefined failures, and UNKNOWN retaining active fields. Existing
four-attempt/fallback, Venice/GMI JSON/SSE, prepaid-refusal, terminal recovery and
late-response fixtures remain separate. These semantic witnesses are
UNRUN_CODE_ONLY_HOSTED; syntax and source anchors are not execution evidence.


Current accounting contract: fixed execution products remain subject to each
operation/source/read/terminal128 window and sync512 separately. Enumerated
PAYLOAD_GRAPH_TRAVERSAL_CONTROL workspace is paid separately on the genuine HTTP
owner's ExactBodyCharge under COMMON96MiB. It is not DATA and has no new arena or
quota. The foreground credential GenerationConsumptionLease remains32MiB; it is
neither a whole-HTTP cap nor workspace credit. Source BodyLease, exact JSON
supplement, foreground credentials, retained generation, HTTP traversal,

arguments below are historical checkpoints; their universal coverage claim is
withdrawn for the explicitly enumerated traversal workspace. They do not become
proof that the remaining fixed windows fit.

Internal graph-processing/runtime helpers, generated snapshots and the pinned
tokenizer are trusted except for the specific hardened boundaries documented
here. Arbitrary unrelated global helper, registry, coercion, cache or tier
mutation is outside qualification. This does not withdraw the supported hostile
I/O-original, byte-operand, Headers, constructor, serialization-hook or metadata
cases. Tenant JSON, inert toJSON, own __proto__, deep/wide native input and the
existing expanded profile are unchanged. No whole-native-heap bound is claimed.

The request capability owns a positive charge in the existing common Worker
body arena. Authentication remains inside the genuine generation owner; its
result is a scalar verdict or an error Response. An early credential/policy
check does not establish provider, selection or session coverage. Those facets
must match the actual retained generation before their later use.

## Original upload custody

The private preparation branch registers an upload owner before constructing a
source or `FixedLengthStream`. It calls the source's original `pipeTo` directly,
observes its original promise immediately, and retains the fixed stream's
readable side for dispatch. The ordinary preparation branch keeps its existing
`pipeThrough` behavior.

Unused preparation initiates owned abort before joining the pipe. A source
created before a later factory fails receives its own original cancellation.
A source locked without an owned original pipe has unknown custody: its scope
and funding stay held. Successful fetch headers, a public response, a wrapper
EOF, and a timer are not substitutes for original pipe settlement.

The final request consumer joins the original roots before clearing source,
fixed-stream, listener and promise aliases. Independent upload failures retain
their origins, including `undefined` and two occurrences of the same Error.
The public response must not wait on its own encompassing completion.

Upload scopes are separate from the logical delivery/accounting completion.
Unused preparation cancels immediately. A dispatched upload can continue while
logical HTTP consumers remain active. When those consumers finish, retirement
initiates cancellation of any still-unsettled upload before joining its original
pipe and scope. Otherwise a provider response that finishes without consuming
the upload could make the upload wait on its own encompassing completion. A
held original cancellation still holds the owner and funding; a public SSE
prefix does not settle it. New Node and native witnesses exercise this dispatched
case, including an independent `undefined` cancellation failure. They are unrun.

## Outstanding closure

The request and upload metadata allowances are provisional implementation
components, not a completed DATA/CONTROL proof. The full HTTP overlap with
generation hydration, selection, foreground credentials, accounting, retention
and delivery still requires an actual prospective inventory under the existing
ceilings. This checkpoint grants no new arena, control slots, physical-heap
bound, or default-route coverage.

The private Venice branch now preserves the native-response descriptor while
using its genuine foreground owner for response intake and delivery. It does
not call the ordinary 64 MiB JSON or 16 MiB SSE lease acquisition. The new
codec and revalidation components share one additive ledger under that owner's
existing 32 MiB charge. The implemented component reservations below still
require complete DATA and CONTROL closure; wiring and static checks do not
establish whole-vertical fit or runtime qualification.

The full-admission bridge can omit selection coverage for legacy completeness
or a final stale-plan drop. The private provider command permits reacquisition
only after a successful reply has BOTH selection and snapshots absent, and after
genuine current authentication, requested-facet, policy, model, connection and
unavailability checks. It does not infer the omission's cause. It owns ordered
default-key and KV reads, reuses the pure
candidate planner, and reserves result storage before one `/grant-pools/select`.
Nonrecording selection makes no counting RPC; a lost selection reply is uncertain
and is not replayed. It never writes a second `/grant-pools/record` for that choice.
The immutable generation is unchanged. A complete empty/unusable selection and
its requested snapshot are authoritative; they do not trigger fallback. A lost
admission reply or failed read/cleanup is terminal, never a successful omission.
The no-prior-selection proof covers selection journal entries only: earlier
credential/policy seeds and projection publication can still have committed.

The Worker candidate phase keeps its own cumulative seven-counter allowance
across optional pool resolution and every original KV record. It does not reset
or reuse the generation's inherited admission ledger. The selection reply keeps
its existing separate bound. Runtime observations are looked up by actual
authority, key and `grantRevision`, and detached under positive funding before
another await can replace their independent owner. This adapter and its SQLite
witnesses remain source-only implementation work, not runtime qualification.

Authored upload witnesses cover a 1 MiB ASCII Chat serialization, unused and

while cancellation remains held. They are not evidence that the full UNIFIED
entry executes. Local dependency-backed semantics remain unrun; local checks
are syntax and source checks only.

The current local selection checkpoint also covers nonrecording projected choices without consuming the initial-selection receipt. Legacy feedback is admitted through the actual selected owner and its `grantRevision`, rather than inferred snapshot membership. Its pending fixed cohort drains in the originating HTTP finalizer after the other original roots settle. A cohort that cannot start remains owned and funded. Authored Node and native KV/SQLite witnesses cover count-once, nonrecording, mixed candidate order, and revision-isolated pending observations; execution is still pending hosted qualification.

## Compiled HTTP checkpoint

`privateOwnedHttpChat` is a named build entry. It accepts only POST to
`/v1/chat/completions`; the default export and public configuration do not select
it. Catalog discovery in the fixture uses the ordinary control-plane handler.
The pinned OpenClaw plugin selects its actual unified Chat transport from that
catalog. One client matrix row is added; all prior rows remain.

Foreground credentials have a separate, genuine capability. The request reserves
its 32 MiB temporary component after successful secret preverification and before

selection. The known manifest/preparation component is reserved at that same
preverification boundary and consumed once by the initial attempt. Alternates
fund their own component before their new selection effect. A private destination hit borrows the same immutable credential graph;
misses use original materialization. Optional destination refusal leaves the
temporary owner usable. Prepared callbacks retain that owner through actual
delivery, accounting and archive roots. The finalizer clears proxy aliases before
credential, selected-record and authentication owners. A failed last-consumer
handoff holds funding instead of releasing a dependent owner.

The preliminary intake authentication exposes only a scalar verdict. If its
genuine coverage is identity-only, it has no children and no identity has been
bound to the proxy, the upgrade clears every preliminary capability/coverage
alias before retiring that owner. The replacement still performs secret
preverification and full current admission. No stale preliminary identity is a
fallback. This avoids three simultaneous 32 MiB owners for an OFF large-body
request, which would exceed the common 96 MiB before counting request data.
Bound or child-bearing owners keep their original last-consumer lifetime.
Node and actual-native OFF one-MiB witnesses inspect the two admission RPCs and
positive funding at the second call; execution remains pending hosted gates.

The two shallow selection carriers prospectively charge every copied string key,
primitive and string value occurrence, with the existing 16384-unit model bound.
Nested plain graphs remain borrowed from the intake owner. This component is not
a proof for the later serializer, accounting or complete HTTP phase overlap.
Those DATA and CONTROL tables are still open; the checkpoint is not a candidate
for publication. Node and native tests have only received static syntax checks.

## Intake and delivery checkpoint

The private native intake reserves a 131072-byte read component before acquiring
its original BYOB reader. Each original read supplies a fresh 16384-byte backing;
the returned whole backing is checked, including short views. Original provided
and returned backings remain covered across any authentication await. A short
chunk receives an exact-size retained copy only after prospective admission;
the larger original backing is cleared after that iteration. Zero-byte chunks
retain no history. Node fixtures use explicit native-byte producers; hosted
Workerd must qualify the actual native request boundary. There is no default-
reader fallback or change to the ordinary entrypoint. This is a bounded read
workspace, not a lower wire-size or JSON profile.

The existing positive `BodyLease` credit is counted once; an exact common
arena charge supplements that credit before decode or parsed-graph factories.
Small inputs keep native `JSON.parse`, including deep unknown data, duplicate
last-wins fields and own `__proto__` data. A constant-state lexical pass counts
all source container, member, array-element, primitive and decoded string-unit
occurrences before parsing. It creates no key vector, graph or decoded string.
For valid JSON, array elements equal commas plus nonempty containers minus
members. The prospective graph component is `8*(C+2M+E+N)+2U`; duplicate source
members are conservatively included. This is logical application accounting,
not a native-parser allocation or physical-heap estimate.

The full decoded text and prospective graph overlap stays funded through parse.
Only after text/buffer/index aliases clear may the exact supplement shrink.
Expanded input still uses the existing structural limits and parser; its graph,
current scalar concatenation, decoder chunk and original backing are funded
before each feed. No source counter becomes payload credit. The intake does not
change ordinary JSON grammar or impose a new source-size limit.

The private caller also partitions decoded feeds at relative nesting growth 16.
The pinned `stream-json` 3.8.0 tokenizer returns a whole token batch before the
existing consumer checks depth. Its parent stack therefore needs a bound before
that consumer runs: at most 128 accepted parents plus 16 pending parents. The
partition scans quotes and escapes with two booleans and scalar cursors; it adds
no per-depth frame. The ordinary tokenizer/parser and their callers are unchanged.
Inputs with several simultaneous syntax/structure faults can encounter a
different first fault at a different feed boundary, as with ordinary chunking.
Valid depth 128 remains accepted; depth 129 still reaches the existing refusal.

Before the tokenizer factory/feed, an additional 524288-byte DATA component
covers its whole batch, carried lexeme, decoder slice, scalar-merge overlap and
bounded regex products. A feed has at most 4096 decoded units plus the tokenizer's
16-unit lookahead and one held surrogate. Per unit, an upper bound is one dynamic
two-field chunk record (80 logical bytes excluding its value units), three token
reference cells (24 bytes), and two bytes of value units: 435978 bytes for 4113
units. The remaining component covers simultaneous merge/buffer/regex data.
This is prospective passive DATA; the parser frames, tokenizer parent stack and
execution products remain in the open whole-vertical CONTROL inventory.
The exact dependency source is the frozen 3.8.0 parser blob
`afeed37cef5b9338b50c1594d17478ec83d22f59` in upstream commit
`0f70a661308d12973b63f1e7ec23c396265a5361`.

The private response consumer observes original read, first cancellation and
unlock origins separately. Independent `undefined` or shared-Error rejections
remain separate occurrences. A native errored stream's cancellation can instead
propagate its already-observed read failure without calling the underlying cancel
algorithm; that propagation is classified by original state, never reason
equality. Reader-closed and retirement joins clear original aliases before the
request can release its funding. Unknown locks keep the owner funded.

HTTP selection and runtime feedback reserve their pending work but defer its
transport to the originating finalizer. After delivery/upload/account/archive
roots settle, proxy aliases and selected/credential/authentication consumers
clear before the finalizer starts its own fixed pending cohorts. Pending
capabilities independently retain their parent generation. This avoids starting
feedback while full HTTP consumer graphs are still retained; it is not a claim
that the complete simultaneous CONTROL inventory is closed.

The authority's final dependency check can remove selection and snapshots,
producing the same both-absent shape as legacy completeness fallback. The owned
command deliberately relies on their shared successful-omission contract, not
an invented reason marker. It uses final returned policy/tenant/pool fields and
never replays full admission to classify the omission. Authority source remains
unchanged. Whole-vertical numerical funding and CONTROL closure remain open.

The generation owner registers fixed verifier scalars for the unchanged GMI and
Venice control consumers. The receiving command checks the exact genuine owner,
identity and digest before funding or constructing a proof; public caller graphs
cannot supply that provenance. The request retains every accepted identity proof
through delivery and other original roots, then clears those map entries before
the authentication owners retire. Existing provider-specific authorization and
credential inspections still execute. A covered generation/credential hit does
not waive those mandatory fresh provider controls.


## Private Chat pricing scope

The ninth production seam is `pricing.ts`. A genuine HTTP invocation registers
its actual transformed `openai.chat_completions` body before full admission can
commit an initial choice. The scope is entered only around a named synchronous
accounting or estimation call and is removed in its cleanup. It covers the early

The ordinary estimator and other protocol branches retain their existing path.

One passive array stores only references to the still-funded input graph. Before
each growth, its maximum simultaneous cells Q receive `8*(1+Q)` logical bytes
from the same positive HTTP charge. There are no per-depth call frames, key
arrays, continuation records, suspended iterators or closures. Fixed plan/scope
metadata has a positive 4096-byte component; the two lazy registry maps have a
separate exact 512-byte common-arena owner. All plan and body aliases clear before
that registry or the request charge can retire. These are logical accounting
components, not JavaScript object sizes or a whole-vertical CONTROL proof.

The scalar measurement includes JSON punctuation, quoted keys, omitted object
members, array nulls, finite/nonfinite numbers, control escapes, UTF8 code points
and escaped lone surrogates. It does not serialize the whole body for pricing.
The original dispatch serializer and upload remain separate consumers requiring
their own prospective funding. Media detection keeps the exact input/message
content/tool roots, type list and property-presence checks, including null values.
Cache checks cover the whole JSON graph; a one-hour match returns its rate without
also maximizing the five-minute rate. Other pricing gaps, manifest rates, fixed

Authored pricing witnesses use real credential/admission/budget fixtures and the
